# National Park Service reports on the 1863 Gettysburg landscape: index

Workstream bt-web, task nps-landscape. Generated by `scripts/build_sources.py` from `catalogue.json`, `source_meta.json` and the local manifest; do not edit by hand.

- Acquired: 239 documents and pages (2519.4 MB as served). Not acquired: 1 (listed at the end with the reason).
- Texts: `tmp/library/web/nps/<ID>/<ID>.txt` (reading order) and `<ID>.layout.txt` (layout), one `=== PAGE n ===` marker per PDF page (n is the PDF page index, not the printed page; in the NPS-HABS-* texts page 0 is the Library of Congress record of the survey, with its notes verbatim). The tree is untracked; `scripts/acquire.py` rebuilds it from `catalogue.json` and the hashes below detect a changed file.
- Rights basis: `United States government work` for reports written by or for the National Park Service; a different basis is stated on the entry where the document itself shows another author or owner.
- Author, date and series are taken from the document itself (title page or cover as saved); `not stated` means the saved text shows none.
- "Feature states" says which dates the descriptions in the document refer to. A modern survey describes modern or reconstructed features unless it says otherwise; see `extracts/` for the dated passages.

Extract sheets: `extracts/fields-woodlots.md`, `extracts/fences.md`, `extracts/farm-buildings.md`, `extracts/hospitals.md`, `extracts/monuments-later.md`, `extracts/other-landscape.md`; machine-readable: `extracts/extracts.jsonl`.

How an extract is bound to its page: `quote` is cut from the saved text (never retyped; a cleaned quote repairs only the damaged glyphs of the text layer and lists each repair); `context_quote` (page `context_pdf_page`) is a second verbatim passage, usually the entry heading or a list head, that carries a name, date or number the claim needs; every number of a claim stands in one of the two, and `scripts/verify_extracts.py` re-checks both against the saved text. A claim that itself states a hedge is tier inferred; a passage that mixes an 1863 state with a later change is split into two extracts (use direct, and use indirect or none).
